<meta>This is the title page of a 1743 work by Johann Gottfried Jugel. It introduces a dialogue between "Mercury" (representing alchemical theory and volatility) and a "Smelter" (representing practical labor), bridging the gap between mystical alchemy and the industrial reality of 18th-century German mining.</meta>

# Philosophical Dialogue
->between the<-
<insert>Library of the Gymnasium in Hildburghausen</insert> <note>This refers to a library stamp from a secondary school in Hildburghausen, Germany.</note>
# **Flying Mercury**
<note>original: "Fliegenden Mercurium". In alchemy, Mercury often symbolizes the spirit of transformation or the volatile principle of metals.</note>
->and a common<-
# **Smelter,**
Wherein is perfectly and thoroughly shown and
demonstrated the true and natural secret, never before discovered 
and highly necessary for the great World-Economy, 
<note>In this period, "Economy" referred to the management and orderly system of the natural world and its resources.</note>
## **Art of Roasting and Smelting,**
<term>Roasting and Smelting: "Roasting" is the process of heating ore to remove impurities like sulfur, while "smelting" involves melting the ore to extract the base metal.</term>
How to ripen and fix all volatile and subtly metal-bearing types of ore and rock, 
according to the required circumstances, with their proper charges;
Together with a complete and thorough report
## **Of Nature and the Creature,**
How these, through the earthly course of all time, may be dissolved again by the spirit of their birth and return to their first essence,
## **As a Manifest Proof in the Mineral Kingdom**
<note>original: "Regnum Minerale"</note>
carried out; these secret truths have never been so clearly discovered and brought to light through the foundations of nature.
Wherein is also further cited how the current roasting and
smelting works are organized and operated in most of the principal mountains of Germany.
## **Toward the thorough knowledge of true nature, the exploration**
of her secrets, and the elevation of noble mining in all lands,
discovered through his own experiments
->by<-
## **Johann Gottfried Jugel.**
---
->B E R L I N,<-
->To be found with Johann Andreas Rüdiger, privileged bookseller, 1743.<-

<summary>This title page introduces a treatise on metallurgy and alchemy, presented as a dialogue between the spirit of Mercury and a practical smelter. It promises to reveal secret techniques for processing ores and explains the natural lifecycle of minerals within the "Mineral Kingdom."</summary>
<keywords>Johann Gottfried Jugel, alchemy, mining, metallurgy, roasting and smelting, Berlin 1743, Mineral Kingdom, natural philosophy</keywords>

# Preface.

->Good Fortune!<-
<note>original: "Glück auf," the traditional German miners' greeting, wishing for the earth to open and reveal its treasures.</note>
->Noble, art-loving, and kind<-
->Reader!<-

That all mere efforts of our own, without a thorough knowledge of Nature, are nothing but vain and futile things in this matter, can be sufficiently proven to us by the fixation <term>fixation: the alchemical process of "fixing" a substance to make it stable and resistant to fire</term> or maturation <term>maturation: the historical belief that metals "ripen" like fruit within the earth over long periods of time</term> of those volatile and subtle metal-bearing ores, which has been sought in so many places across Germany for a quite a long time; and while such a task is indeed possible and would be of indescribably magnificent benefit,

<summary>In this preface, the author warns that human effort in mining and alchemy is futile without a deep understanding of Nature's own processes, specifically regarding the maturation of ores.</summary>
<keywords>mining greeting, fixation, maturation of metals, Germany, natural philosophy, metallurgy</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page concludes the preface. It transitions from the medical analogy of the previous page to the author's intent for the treatise: to provide a practical guide for smelters that aligns with the "laws of nature" rather than just rote labor.</meta>

# Preface.

know <note>The text continues from the previous page's unfinished sentence: "...which, however, you should know..."</note> that it is not every man’s work to accomplish such things, because it cannot be done as easily as one might imagine. Rather, it requires patience along with diligent investigation of the ores, as I intend to teach and demonstrate quite clearly and according to the laws of nature in this treatise <note>original: "Tractat"</note>. Even a common smelter (provided he understands his furnace) will be able to adapt to it quite easily. He will learn to make a tangible distinction between these teachings of nature <note>original: "Natur-Lehren"</note> and the usual labors and tasks performed in the works. My goal is that the path of nature in the metallic kingdom <note>A common alchemical and metallurgical concept viewing metals as part of a living "kingdom" with its own growth and laws</note> may be paved, and thereby a better profit <note>original: "Nußen," referring both to the yield of metal and the financial gain</note> may be achieved than is otherwise possible. This I heartily wish for, and I humbly recommend myself to the favor of the most gracious reader.

Berlin,
Written the 12th of December,
1742.
->the AUTHOR.<-

<summary>The author concludes his preface by stating that while metallurgy requires patience and study, his treatise will provide a clear, "natural" method that even a common smelter can follow. He aims to improve the efficiency and profitability of ore processing by following the laws of the "metallic kingdom."</summary>
<keywords>metallurgy, natural philosophy, smelting, metallic kingdom, 18th-century science, Berlin, ore processing</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>The previous page concluded the author's preface, dated 1742, in which he promised to explain the "natural" laws of metallurgy to assist practical smelters. This page begins the main text, structured as a dialogue—a popular educational format of the era—between the allegorical figure of Mercury and a Smelter.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut headpiece featuring symmetrical scrolling foliage and floral motifs. Two large C-shaped scrolls on either side are adorned with hanging tassels, joined in the center by a vertical floral element and a base of leaves.</image-desc>

->*Mercury.*<-

Good fortune to you! <note>original: "Glück auf!", the traditional German greeting used by miners and metallurgists since the 16th century, wishing for the "opening" of the ore veins and a safe return from the mine.</note> My dear smelter, good fortune to you! I see that you are occupied with many tasks here in your smelting hut; I pray you, do not take it ill if I, with my unexpected visit, should be a hindrance to you now.

*Smelter.* What kind of unexpected and unhoped-for visit is this? And who are you, coming upon me so suddenly in my hut? Are you a man, or a spirit, or do you come from the realm of the dead? Or what else should I make of you?

*Mercury.* I am neither man nor spirit, nor do I come from the realm of the dead; rather, I am that man of whom you perhaps have heard much—he who is everywhere persecuted and driven away, martyred and tormented. <note>In the alchemical tradition, Mercury (representing both the metal quicksilver and a philosophical principle) is often described as a "fugitive" who must be "caught" or "fixed," and who undergoes "torture" through the heat and acids of chemical processes.</note> I am alive, yet banished from the land of the living; I have therefore now turned to you, so that I might seek my refuge here and rest a little.

*Smelter.* My dear man, whoever you may be, I tell you only this: that you here

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Mercury and the Smelter from page 9. On the previous page, Mercury introduced himself as a persecuted figure seeking refuge. Here, the Smelter warns Mercury that his workshop is a place of constant labor, while Mercury clarifies that he seeks rest only from the "torment" of heat and chemical manipulation.</meta>

have come to a poor place, and will enjoy little rest in my hut: for here there is work early and late, day and night, as you shall see; therefore I advise you, rather turn yourself elsewhere.

*Mercury.* My dear *Smelter*, it is not to be understood that I wish to take rest with you through sleeping and laziness; I know well that nothing but wakefulness counts here. I only wish to enjoy this rest: that one does not *vex* <note>original: *vexire*; to harass or torment. In metallurgical and alchemical contexts, "vexing" a substance refers to the aggressive manipulation of it through heat and chemicals.</note> me in my body, and through singeing and burning, boiling and roasting, distress my soul. For I have no natural rest, and therefore cannot sleep, because I am of more spiritual than physical properties. <note>Mercury explains that as a "philosophical" or "spiritual" substance, he does not require sleep like a human, but he seeks a reprieve from the violent processes of traditional metallurgy.</note>

*Smelter.* Well, I saw right away that you must be a strange fellow, because you entered through my locked door in a completely unexpected manner; for that reason, I took you for a ghost come from the realm of the dead, and I made more than ten holy crosses <note>The Smelter is describing making the "sign of the cross," a traditional protective gesture against spirits or demons.</note> before you, so that you should not come too near me—yet it would not help at all.

*Mercury.* You are not to take me for a ghost, much less worry that I will cause you any harm; for I offend no one, but am at all times more helpful than harmful to mankind—if only they would leave me in peace, and not deal and trade with me so cruelly in all places that it might often move a stone to pity.

**Smelter.** Well then, do tell me, my dear friend, who you are, and who has done you so much harm that you complain about it in so many ways; rest assured that no harm shall come to you with me, for I am a peaceable man and offend no person, and my roasting- and smelting-fires <term>Röst- und Schmelz-Feuer: metallurgical processes where ore is heated to remove sulfur (roasting) and then melted to extract metal (smelting).</term> will likewise do you no harm, even if they be ever so large and usually have a most cruel stench.

**Mercury.** My dear Smelter, I am Mercury, and I come from the hospital <term>Lazareth: traditionally a hospital for those with contagious diseases; here, Mercury uses it to describe the alchemists' laboratories as places where he was "wounded."</term> of the alchemists, who have left me little peace; rather, as long as they had me in their power, they treated me so miserably that I am still much too weak to recount it.

**Smelter.** Aha! So you are that man; I have certainly heard much about you, for almost the whole world has something to say of you, and to this day no one can quite reconcile themselves to your strange form and properties, because everyone finds themselves deceived by you in the end; therefore I have often heard such things about you that it would likely bring you no great joy to hear them.

**Mercury.** Just tell me, my dear Smelter, who then has spoken of me, and whom am I supposed to have ever offended? Perhaps I can show you in this matter that I have been much wronged: for in one’s absence, people may say much about a person, which often does not hold up <note>original: *den Stich nicht hält*; an idiom meaning "to not withstand scrutiny" or "to be proven false."</note> when both parties are present.

**Smelter.** No, no, my mine- and foundry-officers <term>Berg- und Hütten-Officirers: the administrative and technical supervisors of a mining district and its processing plants.</term> all complain about you, that you are such a

✳ o ✳
<meta>catchword: that</meta>

[that you] are a fleeting bird <note>original: *flüchtiger Vogel*; a metaphor for mercury's volatility and its association with the winged messenger god.</note>, (for they are almost all alchemists,) and when they think they have bound you completely, and are now masters over you, you fly away again in an unexpected manner and leave an empty dwelling behind; I certainly would not want to have much to do with you.

*Mercurius.* Listen, my dear Smelter, how clumsily these people deal with me; it is no wonder that I escape them and fly away: for through their manifold sublimation <term>Sublimation: the process of turning a solid substance into vapor through heat. In alchemy, this was often used for purification, but Mercury complains it only makes him more "volatile" or likely to evaporate.</term> they only provide me with more new feathers, instead of cutting them off as they should. For this is surely certain: that I am of a very strange kind and properties, and require for my right preparation also rare people who thoroughly understand my nature and know how to handle everything in the right place in their works, so that they may reach the goal of their desire through me. But the way the blind world now plays its game with me, it is not possible to accomplish anything good thereby, nor to attain anything perfect; instead, I always fly away from them again.

*Schmeltzer.* Do not tell me that; our Smeltery Manager <term>Hütten-Verwalter: the administrative head of a metallurgical works.</term> is certainly such a man who has gone through the chemical school <note>original: *Chymische Schule*; in this era, "chemistry" and "alchemy" were often used interchangeably.</note> through and through, as is also our Mining Physician <term>Berg-Physicus: a state-appointed doctor and technical official who oversaw the health and regulations of a mining district.</term>; they all do it as it ought to be done, yet it still never helps; it all lies solely with you.

*Mercurius.* I serve everyone quite willingly, and am inclined to serve everyone—indeed, I am created for the purpose of serving everyone—but one must begin with me in the right way. Nature has chosen wings for me; those very wings one must seek to cut off,

<summary>Mercury explains that alchemists fail to "fix" or stabilize him because their methods—specifically sublimation—actually increase his volatility rather than taming it. The Smelter remains skeptical, noting that even highly educated local mining officials have failed to master the substance.</summary>

that I can serve on foot and manage my duties: for as long as I am still volatile—that is, appearing to you with my wings—you can take little comfort in my refinement <note>original: *Verbesserung*; in alchemy, this refers to the "fixing" of a volatile substance into a stable, non-evaporating form.</note>, for as a man is, so will he also bring his works to completion with me, and whoever does not yet rightly understand my nature accomplishes nothing at all with me.

**Smelter.** This thing I do not quite understand; I also do not know where you are supposed to have your wings, for I see none on you. I certainly thought, if there were no other tricks <note>original: *Mucken*; quirks, whims, or hidden difficulties.</note> behind you than these, that taking away your wings should be easy to do. But I think you can also make yourself invisible, because you appeared to my sight just now through my locked door; that thing could not have happened in a natural way. I can never imagine it; you must be a truly flighty bird <note>original: *leichtfertiger Vogel*; a play on words meaning both "fickle person" and a literal bird, referring to mercury's volatility.</note>, especially since you have already deceived so many people with your speed.

**Mercury.** So you still cannot forget that I came to you through your locked door, and therefore you conclude I might even be able to practice witchcraft and use such an art to lead people astray? No, my dear Smelter, this great speed and agility of mine comes from natural causes. Do you not yet know my spiritual properties <term>Spiritual properties: In early chemistry and alchemy, "spirit" (*spiritus*) referred to the volatile, vaporous, or gaseous state of a substance. Mercury is explaining that his ability to permeate barriers is a physical trait of his vapor, not magic.</term>, even though you have dealt with me and my kind for so long? This seems very strange to me: oh, where will all your labors lead, if you do not know how to deal with me rightly in both great and small fires? Consider everything you do and what

<meta>This page continues the dialogue from page 13, where Mercury was explaining that his volatile nature is not a trick, but a natural spiritual property that requires the skill of the worker to master. The Smelter remains deeply skeptical of Mercury’s utility in practical metallurgy.</meta>

-> * ☉ * <-

you have before your hearts <note>The catchword from the previous page suggests "hearts" (Herzen) completes the opening thought.</note> in all your works, so that you may finally enjoy something fruitful from them.

*Smelter.* Oh yes, you perhaps think I am also an alchemist, my dear *Mercury*, and would also let myself be led on and deceived, and vexed by your antics? Oh no, I am not so foolish; I have always guarded myself against that. I prefer to take what GOD bestows upon me in my labors, and specifically what I can gain and obtain through my smelting; thus I know for certain that I shall never lack. But with you and your kind, I do not wish to have much to do, for should I rely on you in my smelting-works <note>original: *Schmelz-Arbeiten*; the physical process of extracting metal from ore using heat.</note> and have you with me at all times, I know for certain I would be abandoned enough; for you would bring me more harm than benefit, and take more away from me and make things volatile <note>original: *flüchtig*; literally "flighty," referring to substances that evaporate or vanish when heated.</note>, rather than fixing <term>Fixing: The alchemical process of *fixatio*, turning a volatile, spirit-like substance into a solid form that can withstand fire.</term> and binding them for me as you should; therefore do not even think that I will desire you as an assistant in my affairs.

*Mercury.* You good Smelter, you cannot go without me for even a moment—not without having to deal with me or my kind—even if such a thing remains a hidden matter to you now <note>original: *anitzo*; an archaic German term for "at this time."</note> because of your ignorance, since you do not yet rightly know me in my nature and essence and therefore hold me in great contempt. If you were only properly acquainted with me and understood my secret workings in your fire-works <term>Fire-works: *Feuer-Arbeiten* refers to any metallurgical or chemical process involving high heat and furnaces.</term> from their foundation, you would certainly prattle quite differently. I moreover pity this darkened simplicity of yours...

hearts, and have a merciful compassion for your ignorance. <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "...because of your ignorance, sin [which] you have before your hearts."</note>

**Smelter.** Yes, yes, speak as you will; I shall certainly guard myself against you, so that I have little to do with you. I have often laughed at my masters for being so simple as to still believe a single word you say, since I have seen and heard how many times you have miserably deceived them and led them into quite adventurous errors <note>original: *abendtheuerlich veriret*; suggesting being led into wild, nonsensical, or wandering paths of logic.</note>. Yet they still have patience with you and let themselves be led astray by you all over again; I would not have so much patience for it. But wait, I must now tend to my furnace again and see to my work, so that I do not miss anything because of this chatter; for this ore-roasting <term>Ore-roasting: The process of heating ore (Erz-Röstung) to a high temperature to burn off impurities like sulfur or arsenic before the actual smelting begins.</term> is not my only task here; as you see, I have still more to do.

**Mercury.** It would grieve me, my dear Smelter, if you were to neglect anything in your labors because of our conversation, for I see that you are a diligent man and have so many strange fires around you that one might soon become anxious and fearful in their presence. But what kind of thoughts must you Smelters and Foundry-men <term>Foundry-men: In the original *Hütten-Leute*, referring to workers in a *Hütte* (a smelting works or metallurgical facility).</term> have of me, and what must you take me for, that you have such a great disgust and loathing for me? Yes, you already become anxious and fearful if you only hear my name mentioned—I, who am nevertheless capable, and also quite ready and willing, should you desire it, to accomplish in your roasting and smelting labors that which you, by yourselves...

<summary>The Smelter expresses deep skepticism toward Mercury, mocking his masters for being repeatedly deceived by the spirit’s promises, while Mercury attempts to defend his reputation as a helpful force in metallurgy.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Mercury and the Smelter. Mercury concludes his point about the futility of the chemists' methods before the Smelter recounts seeing Mercury's various physical states in the laboratory, moving from his "fiery" state to his "crystalline" appearance.</meta>

-> ✳ ○ ✳ <-

...surely have to abandon with your supposed sciences. <note>This completes the sentence from the previous page: "Yes... [you will] surely have to abandon [your efforts] with your supposed sciences."</note>

**Smelter.** I believe this well, my dear Mercury, that you are well-versed in fire, and you may no longer shy away from it, because you have bathed in it more often than in water, and among the masters of alchemy, you have often sweated in it so much that you became anxious and fearful, as I also recently saw you in such a fiery form with my masters, that no one could bear to hold you in their hand; I'll bet, they had you right by the head <note>Likely referring to the "head" or "neck" of a distillation vessel, such as an alembic, where mercury vapors collect.</note> then—tell me, how did you feel at that time?

**Mercury.** This is precisely the right method <note>original: *Modus*</note>, which fulfills your will, but not your longing; surely greater follies could not be undertaken with me than your masters have already done: but I assure you, as long as they leave me in this great form of mine, I remain volatile <term>Volatile: In alchemy, "volatile" (*flüchtig*) refers to a substance's tendency to evaporate or "fly away" when heated, rather than remaining "fixed" in the crucible.</term> and march away.

**Smelter.** Ah my dear Mercury, there is certainly not much to be done with you through and through; you appear from the outside just as you wish: for I have seen you with my masters in your true form as well, you shone like a mirror, and were as clear as crystal, also you looked delicate and beautiful, yet when they rejoiced in you most, you fled from them. I for my part have seen and learned so much from you, that you are a true sorcerer <term>Sorcerer: The original *Schwartzkünstler* (literally "black-artist") refers to a practitioner of the dark arts or necromancy; here it implies Mercury's behavior is deceptive and elusive like magic.</term>, and with all your antics you only confuse people; well to those who completely rid themselves of you, and [avoid] your deceitful company...

<summary>The Smelter and Mercury debate the elusive nature of the metal; the Smelter describes Mercury's physical transformations in the furnace and accuses him of being a deceptive sorcerer who leads alchemists astray.</summary>

--- Page 23 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Mercury (represented as the Roman god and the metal quicksilver) and the Smelter. On the previous page, the Smelter described Mercury’s beauty and his tendency to "fly away" or evaporate during experiments. Mercury begins this page by warning that human cleverness alone is insufficient to master him.</meta>

* 0 *

...avoid it, for one finds oneself cheated by you from front to back, no matter how clever one wishes to be.

*Mercurius.* My dear Smelter, this work is far from changing my form; I still look just as pure and shiny as I ever did. For as long as I am not forced out of my running form <note>Mercury's "running form" refers to its natural state as a liquid metal, often called quicksilver.</note>, and my innermost is turned outward—this is what is meant by "clipping my wings" <note>A famous alchemical metaphor. Clipping the wings of Mercury represents "fixing" the metal, or transforming it from a volatile liquid that evaporates into a stable solid.</note>—and I appear to you in my young **Crystalline** form, entirely pure and spiritual, then nothing can be accomplished with me. For those things which are added by you in an entirely irrational manner to give me my beauty or pure form only spoil and stain my innermost being; they are truly more harmful than useful to me, and I am only prevented from fulfilling what you should naturally require of me. You will certainly achieve nothing with me through your own cleverness, for unless you learn to recognize me rightly and seek to gain something through natural means and ways, you may wait for all eternity for anything profitable from me. I can tolerate neither too much cold nor too much heat; instead, whoever hopes to make something of me must hit upon the right middle path.

*Schmelzer.* Ah, my masters have turned your gray coat around so many times and employed so many **Laborants** <term>Laborants: Laboratory assistants or workers who performed the manual tasks of chemical experiments, such as tending furnaces, under the direction of a master alchemist.</term> who have **sublimated** <term>Sublimation: The process of heating a substance until it turns into vapor and then collects again as a solid. In alchemy, this was used to "purify" mercury.</term> and **precipitated** <term>Precipitation: A process where a solid substance is made to separate from a liquid solution, often changing color during the reaction.</term> you—now white, now yellow, now red—and performed so many futile works on you in such intense fires, that I should believe that in hell <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword "dirs" suggests the next page continues "to you" or "for you" (*dir*).</note>

<summary>Mercury explains that alchemists fail because they use "irrational" additives and extreme temperatures instead of "natural means" to fix his volatile nature. The Smelter recounts the many failed laboratory attempts to transform Mercury through sublimation and precipitation.</summary>

*Mercurius.* This is precisely the right mode, which fulfills your will, but not your desire; certainly, no greater follies could be undertaken with me than what your masters have already done. But I assure you, so long as they leave me in this, my great form, I remain volatile and march away.

*Schmeltzer.* Ah, my dear Mercury, it is certain that, through and through, there is not much to be done with you, however you may appear from the outside. In this context, it refers to a purified, transparent, or solidified state of a mineral.</note>, and you looked delicate and beautiful. **Smelter.** My dear friend, I truly do not know who you are scolding so fiercely, or who has caused you such heartache. **Mercurius.** All those people who labor with me in vain have only themselves to blame, and I am entirely excluded from the fault. To my misfortune, I must repeatedly endure such hellish torture and agony that my very strength and effectiveness might wither away; and yet, if someone were to treat me correctly, I would give of myself entirely through a sweet sense of love. I am not complaining about the noble art of <term>Chemistry</term> <note>original: "Chymie," here referring to alchemy—the "Art" of transforming matter.</note> itself, but only about the useless labors that run contrary to both Art and Nature. <meta>This page continues the dialogue between the personified Mercury and the Smelter. On the previous page, the Smelter argued that violent roasting is necessary to drive off "robber" spirits (impurities like arsenic and sulfur). Mercury begins this page by clarifying that he does not oppose roasting itself, but rather the unrefined manner in which it is typically performed.</meta>

-> * ☉ * <-
<note>The symbol ☉ represents the Sun, traditionally associated with Gold in alchemy, appearing here as a decorative or symbolic header.</note>

*Mercurius.* I do not reject the **roasting** <note>original: "Rösten." The process of heating ore to remove volatile elements like sulfur before the actual smelting begins.</note> even before the **smelting** (of the ores), as it is the sole cause by which your still volatile and often very rich metals may be bound, **matured** <note>original: "ausgezeitiget." An alchemical concept suggesting that metals "grow" or "ripen" toward perfection (gold) within the earth; roasting is seen here as an artificial way to speed up this natural ripening.</note>, and **fixed** <note>original: "figiret." From the Latin *fixatio*, meaning to make a substance stable in fire so it does not evaporate.</note>, provided that such a process is arranged and performed according to their specific kind and property. Indeed, the roasting of ores is where their welfare or woe solely and uniquely resides, as you, being an experienced old smelter, should know quite well. *Smelter.* But pray, tell me, what are you saying now? Consider everything you see and hear from me most precisely; do I then not perform my roasting and smelting works **according to Nature**, or are you perhaps capable of showing me how to do it differently and better? Is this now not natural and **rational** enough? What of it, I am no fool. If it were possible to arrange and **handle** <note>original: "tractiren." To treat or process materials in a laboratory or metallurgical setting.</note> it differently, I would certainly have to know it as well, for I deal with it every day, and it would have long since come into my hands; but as it is, it cannot be done otherwise.

...ing, and that they all originate from one single root, even though they have had to divide themselves into so many different outgrowths through earthly mixtures and necessity, as you now see before your eyes in so many ores, minerals, and metals.

**Smelter.** It is said that through Mercury the first beginning of all things arose, and from him everything still derives its life and growth; I have heard this many times from the alchemists. Therefore, because he is said to be the root of gold, many exert great effort to fix <term>fix</term> <note>original: "figiren." **Mercury.** It is indeed correctly stated that Mercury is the beginning or first essence of all created things, and sustains everything through the powers granted to him; however, this is not to be understood as the alchemical Mercury, or so-called quicksilver (as is nevertheless believed by many). For this is already a specific, finished substance <term>Specificatum</term> within the <term>Mineral Kingdom</term> <note>original: "Regnum Minerale."</note>, and cannot be used for such things—a point you must well understand and grasp. Its power can be recognized by the fact that it alone is capable of achieving what all other imagined things can never do. **Smelter.** How does it happen then, that the minerals, such as *antimony* <note>original: "Antimonium"; a semi-metal often used in alchemical purification</note>, *cobalt* <note>original: "Kobold"; in this era, "kobold" referred to ores that were difficult to smelt and seen as "corrupted" by mountain spirits</note>, *cinnabar* <note>original: "Zinober"; the primary ore of mercury</note>, *orpiment* <note>original Latin: "Aurumpigmentum"; a bright yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment</note>, *vitriol* <note>original: "Victriol"; a group of sulfate minerals, usually green or blue</note>, and the like, sometimes also

Can you grasp this now? I think that this has been explained clearly enough to you, such that you could not desire it more perfectly.

**Mercurius.** When I now consider the labors, especially as they are arranged and performed in the <term>processing</term> <note>original: *Zugutmachung*; the technical process of preparing, refining, and beneficiating ore to make it profitable.</note> and smelting of ores, I could nearly faint away when I must see and experience how they are—in such an innocent manner, without any compassion or mercy—placed into your hellish roasting fires for painful <term>torture</term> <note>original: *tortour*; Mercurius uses the language of the judicial system to describe the violent heat applied to minerals.</note>. There, they are afflicted and tormented against nature, with the total destruction of their nascent being, until all their juice and power <note>original: *Safft und Krafft*; in early modern alchemy, these terms refer to the vital "moisture" or spiritual essence within a metal that allows it to grow or be transformed.</note>, so to speak, has escaped them, and the remaining residue (half-burnt as it is) remains as a dead ash. What benefit can you hope to find in these perverted and corrupted labors? You will enjoy nothing more from them than what those actions have brought about—which is to say, little or often nothing at all. Can you really reach your goal in this way, seeking to improve your ores and thinking to melt silver and gold out of them, by first wanting to destroy and ruin them in such a miserable fashion? What do you think? Are you in the right here? Certainly, you people are against your own interests; if only you would for once consult your five senses on this matter and consider what your ores consist of in their innermost being, and what you should then further do with them, you would finally arrive, step by step, at the true <term>light of nature</term> <note>original: *Lichte der Natur*; a Paracelsian concept referring to the innate wisdom found through the observation of the natural world rather than through traditional books.</note>.

**Schmeltzer.** Everything you have just said is true, my dear Mercurius; it has indeed often made you feel ill at such labors, because they are all truly against all reason and work- <note>The word is interrupted by a page break; the catchword indicates it will continue as "workings" or "effects" (*Würckungen*).</note>

...the **earth-fire** <note>original: *Erd-Feuer*; the internal heat of the earth believed by alchemists to "cook" or ripen minerals into metals.</note>, by excessively driven-up earthly vapors, is very rarely permitted [to complete its work], and therefore these mostly bring forth volatile and imperfect metals and minerals to the surface for us.

**Smelter.** <note>The Smelter represents the practical laboratory worker or miner in this dialogue.</note> Thus I see that Nature within the earth has been mostly hindered in her good intention of perfect action in the **generation** <term>original: *Generation*; the alchemical process of the "birth" or formation of metals within the earth.</term> of these metals, and that she herself is not to blame for so many volatile and imperfect metals and minerals. **Mercury.** It is certainly true that we are currently highly dependent on this now-corrupted earth for our metals, and especially iron, because of its magnificent and excellent usefulness. By these things, mankind—who feels this most severely—should be rightfully stirred to carry a longing for their first beginning: that is, in **GOD**, as the eternal, lovely rest.

**Smelter.** I have now, through this teaching of yours, received a great light regarding the perfect operation of kind Nature; and I see quite clearly from it that all these befalling passions have arisen accidentally through the disorderly operations of the destructive and mixed elements. The Smelter now asks about the origin of complex ores containing multiple metals.</meta>

<page-num>68</page-num>

->*  o  *<-

<meta>catchword: without</meta>

...I become aware of what everything depends upon, which we must now endure in this way. But tell me also, how does this happen, that sometimes an ore carries different metals with it, which also allow themselves to be melted together in the fire and always remain together, such as gold, silver, and copper, and more of the like; for this is indeed a natural, wonderfully mixed essence, which surely did not grow in the earth by mere chance?

**Mercury.** This stems merely from the earthly, heaped-up corruptions of their **magnet** <note>In alchemical philosophy, the "Magnet" is a spiritual attractive force that draws celestial influences into the earth to condense into physical matter.</note>, which curdles together in such earthly, sulfurous soil in entire "nests," while the metal still lies in its **first being** <term>original: *primo ente*; the primal, unformed state of matter before it takes a specific shape</term> and is about to enter into **solidification** <term>original: *coagulation*; the process of a liquid or vapor turning into a solid mass</term>. Now, according to how that same magnet is able to keep itself pure within such gathered or flowed-together nests and can accomplish its work therein with the celestial powers, the metal is then generated. If such a little nest remains further unblemished after the complete solidification of the magnet, until the complete **fixation** <term>original: *fixation*; the alchemical stage where a substance becomes stable and can no longer be evaporated by heat</term> of a metal, then it becomes pure gold. But if the other little nest next to it is subjected to more earthly mixtures, then in its ripening, another and lesser metal arises, such as silver, copper, or iron and the like; indeed, just as the internal powers have been sufficient, so shall be the **offspring** <note>original: *Ausgeburth*; a term used to describe the "birth" or final result of the metallic ripening process.</note>. However, if the magnet is entirely suppressed in some places and completely mixed with too many swollen earthly vapors, then that same little part remains volatile, and

*Mercury.* If you now have such volatile arsenical or sulfurous gold pyrites <note>original: *Gold-Kiese*; iron or copper pyrites containing gold</note> or ores that you intend to enrich through roasting, you must first of all know what kind of roasting fire they can endure, and whether you should have them crushed into meal beforehand or left whole, according to which you must first proceed. If you have a large quantity of such ores, you can make your roasting beds <note>original: *Röst-Stätte*; designated areas where ore was layered with fuel and burned slowly in the open air</note> even larger than usual, so that you may place fifty to a hundred hundredweights <note>original: *Centner*; a historical unit of weight, roughly 100–110 pounds</note> into them at once. You can set up as many of these roasting sites as you need for your continuous smelting, or as the quantity of your ores permits, so that you do not have to wait for them during your smelting work; you only have to ensure, finally, that they are protected from rain and storms. You light this roasting pile and let it burn until you see and notice that your entire batch is burnt through. They must also be well covered on top and set with coal <note>likely charcoal</note> rather than wood, which batch may burn for a month, or even longer. Therefore, I say that you should set up many of them, so that you do not have to wait from one time to the next for them.

*Smelter.* But what then are the means by which I may open up <note>original: *auffschliessen*; in metallurgy, this means to break down the ore's structure to make the metal accessible</note> such ores of mine and, as you have said, free them from the earthiness they carry with them? For since the types of ores and volatile minerals <note>original: *Berggeschicke*; an archaic mining term for minerals, particularly those associated with specific geological formations</note> are so diverse, I truly believe that their ingredients and additives <note>original: *Zuschläge*; materials added to a furnace or roasting pile to assist in purification or melting</note> must also be various (depending on the circumstances), by which they might be purified and fixed <note>original: *figiret*; to "fix" a substance in alchemy means to make a volatile or gaseous element solid and resistant to heat</note>.

*Mercury.* The additives for roasting are indeed diverse, as you can certainly imagine for yourself, according to the many properties of the ores. These materials are also available in almost all places; often one only needs to apply a little effort to prepare them, so that one is a match for the volatile ores, to tame and compel them; only they must be transformed and, following the nature of the earth, purified and separated from their acidic, earthy essence, and turned toward the metals. This you must learn and understand very well; then you can use all salts <note>original: *Salien*; a general term for various mineral salts</note>, whatever their names may be, as well as other ingredients such as quicklime, antimony, coal, furnace soot, sulfur pyrites, and even sand, clay, and street-sludge <note>original: *Gassen-Koth*; literally "street filth," often used in historical recipes for the minerals and nitrates found in city waste</note> (according to their preparation) quite usefully for such fixing of the ores during roasting. And even if all these things seem very poor and lowly in and of themselves, do not let that mislead you in this matter; for you will experience and realize that they are quite masterfully to be...

<meta>catchword: brau- [likely *gebrauchen*, "used"]</meta>

For they all hang together like the links in a chain, and none can be recognized or utilized without the other. When you undertake something with a matter, simply consider its essence—namely, what it consists of—and then its beginning and end, and to what degree it has been brought toward its perfection. Then you will immediately be able to see and grasp how it may be helped toward improvement; certainly, one task will point you toward the next, as they all spring from a single root and meet again in a center point <term>center point (Centro): the central, unifying source or essence in alchemical thought</term>, which is what my entire philosophy aims for.

...manage well enough, since I can still fairly understand their nature and properties: but as for the salts <term>salts (Salien): in alchemical theory, Salt is one of the three primary substances (Tria Prima) along with Sulfur and Mercury, representing the body or the principle of fixity</term>, I do not yet understand what they consist of, nor what is meant by them. **Mercury.** Pay close attention now, my dear Smelter, I will faithfully reveal all of this to you herewith, and do for you what I have not yet done for any person in the world, so that you may become perfect in all parts, and it cannot fail you afterwards in your works. A salt, whatever name it may have, consists of a volatile **acid** <note>original: *Acito*</note> and a fixed **alkali** <note>original: *Alcali*</note>, and without those two parts no salt can exist, nor appear in the form of a salt. Acids in **salts** <term>salts (Salien): in alchemy, these are the solid result of the union between a "spirit" (acid) and a "body" (alkali)</term> always arise purely from **earthy exhalation** <note>original: "irrdischen Ausdämpfung"; refers to the material vapors or dross that solidify into matter</note>, and they form such a salt according to how they were constituted at the time of their mixing. For this reason, they may contain much or little **sulfuric essence** <term>sulfuric essence (Sulphurisches Wesen): the principle of combustibility, oiliness, and soul in a substance</term>, and can be now sweet, now sour, now harsh, or now mild; as you will find for yourself. Just now I have told you what the salts here consist of: since you now know this, you should be wise enough yourself (especially since you have well-considered my previous speeches) to be able to **correct** the acid in them. For you have also heard from my philosophy that all severity and passion (and especially the acidity in salts) arises from earthiness alone; pay close attention, for now I am revealing much to you. If you can now turn these around, and make the **acid** <note>original: "Acitum"</note> in them the **active agent** <note>original: "Agens"</note>, so that the **alkaline** <note>original: "Alcalische"</note>, sour, and severe earthy body can no longer bind them or hold them so firmly captive, then you have hit the mark and reached the end of your desire.

**Smelter.** It is true, and your teachings only now seem truly comprehensible to me, so that I can almost grasp it with my hands. When I know from what **temperament** <note>original: "Complexion"; the specific makeup or constitutional balance of a substance</note> such acids in salts or their sulfurous earthiness consist, and in what degree they are united with the **salt-spirit** <term>salt-spirit (Saltz-Geiste): the volatile, spiritual part of a salt that provides its activity</term>, then the matter could be easily helped. For salts are, after all, not such hard and firmly closed bodies as **metals**, **minerals**, or ores; therefore, it is surely easy to reach them with little effort.

*Schmeltzer.* Silence! For if you had not instructed me so clearly in this, I would hardly have been able to understand it; for you wish to have that **spirit** (within the salts) for the **opening** <note>original: "Aufschliessung"; the alchemical process of dissolving or "unlocking" a mineral to release its inner metal</note> of the ores and minerals, which originally brought them to their form and **maturation** <term>maturation (Zeitigung): the alchemical belief that metals "grow" and ripen in the earth over time, from "imperfect" lead to "perfect" gold</term>. For in his first lovely and **unspecified** <note>original: "unspecificirten"; referring to the "Universal Mercury" before it takes on the specific characteristics of a particular metal or mineral</note> essence, he is invisible to human eyes due to excessive clarity and purity, and can pass through all the **pores** of fleshy bodies, even if they do not feel it. In the earthly and destructive form, however, he appears to us as a steamy, thick, and murky smoke, which we cannot allow to enter us without our total destruction and dissolution, as it is highly harmful and ruinous to all **three kingdoms** <term>three kingdoms (drey Reiche): the traditional division of nature into Animal, Vegetable, and Mineral</term>. This is solely because they do not recognize the ores, nor do they know how to make use of the rich metal hidden secretly within them. For what good does it do a man if he knows of an entire mountain full of the best gold-bearing ores, yet cannot smelt them or extract the rich metal? Thus it goes for many upon such rich mountains: he knows a great treasure is hidden within, yet he can make no more use of it than a **pipe of tobacco**.

...must be true, because people say so in so many places.

**Mercurius.** Certainly, this is true, and it is already known far and wide—indeed, throughout the whole world. But in the Harz, they do not yet know it, for the reason that they neither believe it nor are they able to derive any benefit from it. For these ores are a bit more complex <note>original: "künstlicher," here meaning requiring more "art" or specialized skill</note> to treat than their common **silver ores** before metal can be obtained from them. They do not allow themselves to be singed and burnt, distressed and tortured <note>The author uses vivid, almost alchemical metaphors for the high-heat smelting process, which "tortures" the rock to give up its metal</note> in the way the miners are accustomed to doing; instead, they quickly take flight <note>Likely referring to the gold volatilizing or being lost in the slag due to improper heating</note> and leave an empty nest behind. **Smelter.** So the gold ores of that place are still unknown to the gentlemen of the Harz, and—just as in other places—they do not yet know how to **process them** <note>original: "zu gute zu machen," a historical mining term for refining or extracting value from ore</note>. Indeed, that is precisely where the main foundation of the whole true **mining industry** <term>Berg-Wesen: the collective science and practice of mining and metallurgy</term> lies; and where this is lacking, one lives in the greatest imperfection, as I myself have experienced for a very long time. I have heard more talk and stories of no other mountain range than those of the Harz, yet I have not been there, despite having visited many other mountains. I now feel a very great desire to see them for myself.

*Mercurius.* It is a pity, my dear **Smelter**, that you have not yet visited the **Harz** <note>The Harz is the highest mountain range in Northern Germany, famous for its mining history and folklore</note> as you have just said, for there the most beautiful mountains in Germany are to be found, and many <term>Smelting and Refining Works</term> <note>original: "Schmelz- und Hütten-Werck"</note> are in full swing there. But I believe you may know the **Harz Thalers** and **Guilders** <note>Thalers and Guilders were silver and gold coins used throughout the German states</note> much better than their mountains; as long as you possessed them, you troubled yourself no further with anything else. Yet these coins have also shown you that they sprang from a noble mountain range, and where these came from, even more such metal must be present. We wish for the dear Harz a **continuation** <note>original: "Continuation"</note> of this wealth until the end of the world.

*Schmelzer.* It is true; the Harz money makes the fame of their noble <term>Ore-Mountains</term> <note>original: "Erz-Gebürge"</note> and the <term>Silver-Blessing</term> <note>original: "Silber-Segen," a traditional mining term referring to the God-given abundance of metal found in the earth</note> that flows so richly from them known to the whole world. Their money is minted with such fineness that one can never have enough of it; and because I hope there are many more people like me, I wish the gentlemen of the Harz such <term>Mountain-Blessing</term> <note>original: "Berg-Segen," a synonym for mining prosperity</note> a thousandfold for eternity.

*Mercurius.* Now, regarding the area around the **Brocken** <note>The highest peak in the Harz, often associated with legends of witches</note> and **Kyffhäuser Mountain** <note>original: "Giphäuser Berg"</note>, that same blessing of God—which consists of the best and very rich **gold ores**—has not yet been discovered by the country, because their **smelting** and **processing** <term>Zugutmachung: the art of extracting and refining metal from raw ore</term> has remained a secret until now. For because these ores are all very **volatile** <note>original: "flüchtig," referring to substances that evaporate or escape as gas during heating, a common problem when smelting gold and silver</note> (as high-grade ores tend to be) and yet are of rich content, one cannot deal with them easily because of their sharp, penetrating

*Mercury.* There is no doubt that the **Harz**, and especially the mountains around the **Brocken** and the **Kyffhäuser** <note>original: "Giphäuser," a mountain range in Thuringia</note>, contain rich gold-bearing ores, as is well known to true <term>Ore-Experts</term> <note>original: "Ertz-Kenner"</note>. Specifically, they take the old, weathered <term>Seam-rocks</term> <note>original: "Flötz-Gesteine"</note> that outcrop at the surface; you have ores a hundred times better just a <term>Fathom</term> <note>original: "Lachter-Teuffe." Indeed, in some places, such people are strictly forbidden from carrying away such ores (even if they declare them to be worthless, empty stones), and they are not permitted to show their faces on any mountain, even if the minerals never benefit a soul.

*Smelter.* It is very well known to me that such people are nowhere permitted to carry such stones away from the mountains, despite the stones being of no use to anyone else; this happens because people want to keep everything for themselves. But the **Venetians** always get away with it, for it is said they can make themselves invisible.

*Mercury.* So you believe, then, that the **Venetians** <note>original: "Venetianer." In German folklore, "Venetians" were mysterious, often magical foreign prospectors who supposedly traveled to the German mountains to gather gold and minerals using secret knowledge.</note> can make themselves invisible and carry off whatever they desire; in that regard, you are finally quite right, for they possess great arts. For this reason, I would advise you not to stand in their way, if you wish to enjoy God’s blessing in your own land: for they have (as long as the world has stood) treated **Germany** as their **Treasure Chamber**, and they fetch from it whenever it pleases them. The German must now stand by and watch this, and yet can say nothing against it. This happens because, among thousands, hardly one person believes it, and whoever is not particularly familiar with the mountains knows absolutely nothing about it. 

However, those people who live upon such mountains, and see such **Hackle-makers** <note>original: "Heschelmacher," a folk term for these wandering foreign prospectors.</note> coming and going there with their schemes—and who therefore also know for certain where the right holes are and where they pull out their ores—have this common proverb in use: "The ores are **spoiled**," <note>original: "verthan," implying the ore has been bewitched, spent, or rendered magically inaccessible so that no metal can be extracted.</note> that is, displaced or enchanted, so that no one can melt anything from them anymore. 

Now, if one or another person refuses to believe this, he breaks off some of the rock from their holes himself and goes to the usual **Mining or Smelting Assayers**; but because these ores are very volatile due to their very **subtle** <note>In historical metallurgy, "subtle" (subtil) refers to metals that are very fine, pure, or easily lost during crude smelting processes.</note> and rich gold content, and the **Assay Master** <term>Assay Master</term> <note>original: "Guardein," the official official responsible for testing the purity and value of ores.</note> generally does not understand how to treat them, the matter is finally "proven" right, and it must then be called "spoiled." Yes, indeed, they are spoiled enough for you fine gentlemen, because you can make nothing out of them, which is truly a pitiable thing.

**Smelter.** At the so-called **Fichtel-Berg** <note>The Fichtel Mountains in northeastern Bavaria, a region with a long history of mining for iron, tin, and copper.</note>, where I am well-acquainted, the same thing happens. All the peasants there speak of such **enchanted ores** <note>original: "verthanenen Ertzen." As established on the previous page, this refers to minerals that locals believed had been bewitched or "spent" so that they could no longer be smelted.</note>, as I have heard from many of them; indeed, they know of so many such ores that are carried away from there annually in great quantities. They are very beautiful to look at and very heavy in weight, but no one can melt them or turn them to profit (**therein always lies the rub**) <note>original: "hier lieget allezeit der Hanß im Pfeffer begraben," literally "here Hans lies buried in the pepper," a German idiom meaning "that is the root of the problem."</note>. They have already taken them to all the **assayers** <note>original: "Probirern," specialists who tested ore samples to determine their metal content.</note> and **assay-wardens** <note>original: "Guardeins," official overseers of the mint or mining operations who verified the quality of metals.</note>, but no one knows how to treat them. The wardens give them the answer that the ores are still too **volatile and unripe** <note>original: "flüchtig und unreif." In the mining science of the time, it was believed that metals "grew" in the earth like plants; "unripe" ore was thought to be still in a transitional state and thus difficult to extract.</note>. The peasants, however—who know, and often see and hear, that others melt something from them—believe therefore that the ores are bewitched and enchanted.

**Mercury.** It is exactly the same as on the **Brocken Mountain** <note>The highest peak in the Harz Mountains, legendary for its association with witchcraft and folklore.</note>, the **Giant Mountains** <note>original: "Riesen-Gebürge," the Krkonoše range on the border of the modern-day Czech Republic and Poland.</note>, and other such rich mountain ranges. If only you were acquainted with the Brocken, you would encounter things just as wondrous there as on the Fichtel-Berg. Yet the Fichtel-Berg is also one of the largest and most beautiful mountain ranges in **Germany** for metal-bearing ores, and it is also very well known to me, my dear **Smelter**. For I am always roaming about the world in this way and seeing many things, and whenever I have traveled through the **Harz**, I have always stayed longest on this mountain range. For I assure you, whoever is a true lover of mountains and ores can never get enough of this range, as one gets to see a great number of different, curious ores there.

**Smelter.** But listen, my dear Mercury, were you then not afraid of the witches on the **Brocken Mountain** <note>Also known as the Blocksberg, this peak is the highest in the Harz Mountains and is famously associated in German folklore with witches and the supernatural.</note>, who (as it is said) always let themselves be seen and felt there, and must also come together there every year on **Walpurgis Night** <note>The eve of May 1st, traditionally believed to be the time of a great witches' gathering on the Brocken.</note>, where they then dance, jump, and make merry?

**Mercury.** This vain fable <note>original: "eitele Gedichte," literally "idle poems" or "empty stories." Mercury is dismissing the supernatural folklore as fiction.</note> is heard throughout the whole world regarding the Brocken Mountain, even from so many thousands of people who have never seen it in their lives nor shall ever get to see it. This legend arises because very powerful, rich, and noble metals are contained within that mountain, which—through their violent **exhalations** <note>original: "Ausdünstungen." In early modern mining science, it was believed that minerals produced vapors or "effluvia" that could be seen as glowing lights or felt as heat.</note> and strong **atmospheric vapors** <note>original: "Witterungen," a term used by miners to describe the air and gases found within a mine or emanating from the earth.</note> (as will be well known to you as a miner)—let themselves be seen and felt as flames of fire. These flames form an arc, shoot into the air, and even run along like burning **trails of gunpowder** <note>original: "gezettelt Büchsen-Pulver." Mercury is describing "ignis fatuus" or "will-o'-the-wisps," which miners often interpreted as signs of rich ore veins.</note> following such rich veins. This is especially strongest in the spring and autumn (towards the **equinoxes** <note>original: "Equinoction," from the Latin *aequinoctium*.</note>), and in the evening and at night, these appear very fearful and terrifying to the ignorant. Indeed, if you did not already know the cause, you yourself would be horrified by them. These adventurous appearances—because they return every spring and have often been viewed with wonder by the common man who did not understand their origin—gave cause to accuse the notorious Brocken Mountain of the greatest witchcraft. Yes, because these appearances [return] every spring...

<summary>Mercury explains that the "witches" and supernatural lights on the Brocken Mountain are actually natural phenomena caused by flammable gases and "exhalations" from rich metal deposits. **Mercury.** The ancient sages, who knew this spirit and its enabling power very well, called it "All in All," since they knew no other name to give it in this temporal world; it was also called the <term>Non Ens</term> <note>Latin: "Non-Being" or "Non-Entity." In this context, it refers to a substance that has no physical properties yet contains the potential for all existence.</note> of the philosophers (or a thing that is Nothing and yet is Everything). **Mercury.** Thus you have the beginning and the end of the entire creation (so far as you have grasped my full discourse) quite clearly before your eyes, from which you may see and recognize what every thing consists of, and by what means it has been brought into a tangible being. One of the three traditional kingdoms of nature (Animal, Vegetable, Mineral) studied by natural philosophers.</note> as far as the powers of their capacity in those things reach, and as far as Nature has brought their bodies through her **maturation** <note>original: "Zeitigung." The alchemical belief that metals "grow" and ripen in the earth over time, eventually turning into gold if left undisturbed.</note>: for you can bring no thing higher than Nature has delivered it to you according to its inner essence. ...harmonize <note>original: "moniren." Likely a variant of *harmonieren*, meaning to align or correspond.</note> all together, and it is also easy to recognize in them how closely they are related to one another according to their earthly bodies; but in their innermost part (as you will surely be able to grasp), they are of **one essence** <note>In alchemy, this "one essence" refers to the *Prima Materia*, the fundamental substance from which all things are made.</note>. When, then, their mingled earthiness—which has corrupted them here and mixed and **coagulated** <term>Coagulation: The process of a liquid or vapor becoming a solid, dense body.</term> with them at their birth (because the **universal spirit** <note>original: "allgemeine Geist." A reference to the *Anima Mundi* or World Soul, the vital force that penetrates all matter.</note> was capable of doing so, and this has happened in all created earthly things)—is once again separated from them after their **dissolution** <term>Dissolution: The chemical or spiritual breaking down of a solid back into its primary liquid or vaporous state.</term>, then they must indeed reach their first and pure essence again; understand me correctly: this is quite easy to grasp according to the fundamental basis, through the ways of nature.

**Smelter.** My dear Mercury, I can grasp these high secrets of the working and **generation of nature** <note>The "generation of nature" refers to the natural growth and formation of minerals and metals within the earth, which alchemists sought to replicate in the laboratory.</note>, which you have now revealed to me, in all created and visible things through the grace of **God**, to the extent that I see their life and death, beginning and end, standing quite clearly side by side; especially if I only consider your brief words, which you spoke to me concerning the birth and production of mineral and metallic bodies: namely, how this harmful earthiness mixes with the pure celestial influences or the **first essence** of all things, right at the beginning like a puffed-up, thin vapor, but gradually **coagulates** with it during their generation, and finally (so to speak) becomes a solid, dense body with it.

**Mercury.** Correct, my dear man, you have hit the mark, and [grasped] the high [meaning] of my few words...

In alchemical thought, "Earth" is the element that provides structure but also creates opacity and "death" if not purified by light or spirit.</note> so firmly **coagulated** <term>Coagulated: Here meaning hardened or thickened into a solid, physical form.</term> within them, and have been so cruelly corrupted by its harsh passion <note>In this context, "passion" refers to the suffering or transformation of matter as it is acted upon by external forces.</note>. Here, Mercury refers to the alchemical principle of fluidity and spirit, which becomes "corrupted" or sluggish when mixed with heavy earth.</note>) have done little harm. The purer, brighter, and more transparent these bodies are, the harder they are also **coagulated** by nature, which you can perceive quite clearly in these three aforementioned gemstones. *Mercury.* If you now consider the conception, birth, multiplication, and growth of all earthly things here below, and pay close attention to all their changes, movements, and stirrings, you will learn to see and recognize from this that they all receive their life in one Spirit <note>In alchemical thought, this "Spirit" refers to the *Spiritus Mundi* or World Soul, the vital force that animates all matter.</note>, are sustained by it, and must also finally conclude within it. For without this Spirit and its life, there is otherwise nothing; however, corrupted earthliness has merely placed the same into far too many masked <note>original: "verlarffte." This term suggests a "mask" or a "disguise" that hides the true, divine essence of a substance.</note>, gruesome forms and monstrosities, and thereby spoiled its pure, spiritual, and glorious essence in them and brought its radiance to nothing, so that it lies entirely hidden and locked away within them.

*Smelter.* I see, however, from this high philosophy <note>Here, "philosophy" refers to "Hermetic Philosophy" or alchemy—the study of the hidden laws of nature.</note>, that all these earthly things born here below can finally be freed again from their shameful masking and the corruption of their perfections, and released from every hindrance. I see that this happens just as orderly and naturally as their generation <term>Generation: the process of coming into being or being created</term> and birth have been here; as well as that it must happen and be accomplished by the very same path they traveled in this earthly life toward their tangible, bodily essence. Therefore, life and death, beginning and end, the entrance into and the exit from this life, are one and the same for all created things here; they are accomplished through one Spirit in this earthly time, which I have been able to see and grasp quite thoroughly and perfectly through these teachings of yours.

*Mercury.* Thus you see now in this short conversation what you would likely have never been able to comprehend on your own, and for which you would never have received any guidance from all those empty discourses. To be sure, many learned and brave men have perceived and recognized this great and important chief matter, and thus much effort has been applied therein to perfectly accomplish it. However, because they were minded to perform this purely according to the Scripture <note>Referring to the Holy Bible.</note> without a thorough knowledge of true nature—and thus forgot the one alongside the other—the right and true perfection of this great work has remained obscured until now, and has not broken forth into a perfect light among us (to shine for everyone). Indeed, it has caused more confusion than benefit in theological matters. For it remains an established and correct matter that the high works of nature can never be understood or comprehended without a thoroughly recognized understanding of their effects <note>original: "Würckungen." This refers to the active processes and transformations observed in the natural world.</note>; a point which every true lover of natural secrets should allow to be recommended to them.

*Smelter.* Thus, from the works of nature, the restoration of all things to their first essence <note>original: "erstes Wesen." This refers to the original, pure state of matter before it was "corrupted" or masked by earthly impurities.</note> (as the final will of God) is so clearly

<page-num>118</page-num>

...be the essential effects of nature, how they happen and are carried out, which should rightly have given humans guidance toward higher contemplation and knowledge of the same; but because nowadays (as is well known to you), natural things are not investigated according to their true basis, and therefore their secrets are not found, it is no wonder that we live on into the day with the blind world and must be robbed of all knowledge thereby.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;**Smelter.** It is true that all lack of such high, divine, and natural knowledge lies merely in our ignorance and innate blindness; and unless we cast off this dark and cloudy shadow-work <note>In this context, "shadow-work" (Schatten-Werck) refers to the illusory or superficial understanding of the physical world that obscures divine truth.</note> and learn to recognize and grasp the true knowledge of nature with its high, hidden, and secret effects, we must remain stuck in such blindness and ignorance forever. stand with one another; yet in an unequal state, since now death is a destroyer, but life an upholder of all things.

*Mercurius.* That the life and death of all things are one and the same matter is true: but could you perhaps prove this to me from the fundamental basis of nature? It would be even more difficult for you to contemplate and behold death in its perfect state (that is, within life); however, I will faithfully reveal this to you herewith. The life and preservation of all creatures here below is the great **Sideric Universal World-Spirit** <note>The "Siderische Universal-Welt-Geist" refers to the astral or celestial influence believed by alchemists to animate the physical world, acting as a bridge between the divine and the material.</note>, as you will have heard and understood sufficiently from my previous instruction; this is also at the same time the end and conclusion of all things, so that just as it imparts life and its perfection to all things here below, it also destroys them again. For note, within every inanimate and living creature, there must lie locked and hidden as much of this spirit or true life as was capable of making that creature into a tangible being—that is (as I have told you) hidden within its **magnet** <note>In alchemical thought, a "magnet" is not just a piece of iron, but a specific attractive center or spiritual essence within a substance that draws and holds its vital force.</note>. Through the **enabling power** <note>original: "Vermögungs-Krafft"</note> of such a magnet, the life of the creature is also sustained and preserved, so that as long as it lasts and is kept unshifted, healthy, and fresh within a thing, the vital and healthy powers of such creatures lack nothing: but as soon as it becomes weak and is brought into a sensible suffering through earthly, corrupting impacts of the destructive elements working within them, then it begins its order-

<meta>catchword: ly</meta>
<sig>H 4</sig>

<vocab>Mercury, Sideric Universal World-Spirit, Magnets, Death, Life, Elements, Creature, Enabling Power</vocab>

<summary>Mercury explains that the Sideric Universal World-Spirit is the source of both life and destruction, residing within a "magnet" or spiritual center in all creatures. If this malady is not remedied, and the **magnet** <note>The internal spiritual center that attracts vital force.</note> is not strengthened again and thereby restored to its action, then the creature’s total ruin eventually follows, and death sets in instead of further life: for if the inner spirit of life within the magnet becomes far too weak, and its powers of attraction are entirely destroyed, then the great **Sideric Spirit** <note>The astral or celestial influence of the stars.</note> becomes its master and gains the upper hand over it, drawing it back to itself through its powers; thus, what was once its life now becomes its death, demonstrating (in the fury of the destructive passions) the **antipathy** <note>In alchemical philosophy, "antipathy" is the natural repulsion or discord between substances that leads to their separation or destruction.</note> toward them, and thereby accomplishing the end of all finite created things; and just as this spirit was the life and beginning of them all, so it is also their death, as an example of which I shall present to you with certain and infallible proof the hardest, most fixed, and most compact bodies in the Mineral Kingdom.

**Smelter.** My dear Mercury, I must now interrupt your speech: you say so much herewith that I can indeed well understand what you have pointed toward, and you lead into the most wondrous **kingdom** <note>original: "Regnum"</note> of

<page-num>121</page-num>

nature as a proof; however, how does flesh and blood (as the **Animal Kingdom** <note>The "Animalische Reich" includes all living, breathing creatures, including humans.</note>) correspond to the hard bodies of **metals and ores**? *Mercurius.* Although, as is well known, there is a very noticeable difference between the manifestations of the three earthly kingdoms of nature here below, you should nevertheless know this: all these things created and self-multiplying upon our earth not only come from a single origin, but are also entirely the same in their innermost being. It is the mixed effects of the **elements** (from which the distinction between these three kingdoms arose) that have caused these variations in manifestations; yet in their interior, as already stated, they are closely related and of the same birth, even if they are far removed from one another on their surface (according to their outward changes). Therefore, their birth is of one kind, occurring through a single Spirit; likewise, their death is carried out in the same way, by the very same means that initially gave them life. For if you observe the birth and **generation of metals** <note>Alchemists believed that metals were not dead matter, but "grew" and "generated" within the earth like living organisms.</note>, and how the degrees of their **maturation** <term>Zeitigung: the alchemical process by which "imperfect" metals like lead were thought to slowly ripen into "perfect" gold over centuries within the earth.</term> are worked therein from beginning to end (as I have already mentioned), you will easily be able to grasp their perfection, beginning and end, and life and death within them.

These ores were certainly dead, since they had also entirely lost their metallic luster, which is certainly a visible, natural proof by which to recognize the eventual death of all earthly creatures. To many, this might seem very strange to hear, and they might scold you as an **atheist** for believing such things, because these high **mysteries** <note>original: "Geheimniße"</note> are not interpreted here according to their foundations, but rather according to their own self-concocted fables; and people prefer to believe that which merely sounds good to them, or has been in use for a very long time, even if it rests on no foundation and runs against **God’s** most high ordinances—as is so very often the custom.

For it is this which has now opened the first gate to our glory or transformation through its power, and thereby made the beginning to free the sinful nature and **creature** from its distress and passion—which is the perfection of true **philosophy** <note>The "True Philosophy" refers to the Hermetic art or alchemy, which sought the purification of both soul and matter.</note>.

**The Smelter.** This I can finally now, through the high grace of **God** which He has granted me, understand quite well from your explanations. But how does it stand with mankind, who without a doubt have also received their spirit in this earthly birth from the **sidereal forces** <term>sidereal: relating to the stars or constellations; in alchemy, this refers to the astral influences that shape the physical world</term>, and thus are also assigned to one of these three kingdoms, and especially to the **animal realm** <note>original: "Animalische"</note>, because we are also surrounded with flesh and blood (like other **creatures** of this realm) and are therefore, like them, subject to earthly decay down here?

**Mercury.** With mankind, the matter is finally quite different, because their souls (or the inner man) descend from a far higher birth according to their first essence, and as the **Scripture** says, are born of **God**. It is only after the soul’s separation, and after the darkening of its **principle** <note>original: "Principii"; here meaning the fundamental spiritual source or beginning of a being</note>, that it is cast out into such a fleshly dwelling of the earthly body until its redemption (or restoration to **God**). Thus, humans belong to the **animal kingdom** according to their body (but not according to the soul), though with a special condition. However, I will speak no more of this high mystery with you now, because it does not suit our **mineral discourse** <note>original: "Mineralischen Discours"; a technical discussion focused on metals and stones</note>. Through this dialogue, I have only presented the beginning and end of all **nature** and **creature** in this earthly state for you to contemplate, so that you might form a fundamental concept of their secret workings from it, and then be able to follow them correctly in all your labors and operations.

If you can now find and recognize the thorough understanding (which is hidden within this) then you will also see and experience the true nature of man's earthly life and death here below, and thereby learn to understand their final return to **GOD**.

However, I am not inclined to cite it here, but rather wish to leave it to those who are not yet capable of understanding Nature's own conviction and must instead bicker with the **Scribes** <term>Scribes: "Schrifftgelehrten," referring to scholars or theologians who rely strictly on textual tradition rather than direct experience or observation of the natural world</term> over such matters, for they still lack the recognition of Nature’s foundation and her divine workings, and therefore do not truly know what they ought to believe. 

I, however, my dear **Smelter**, do not concern myself with such quarreling, because I recognize in the highest Truth (which is **GOD** Himself) and know for certain that the workings of Nature, with all her secrets, correspond exactly with Holy Scripture. Neither can deviate from the other, as one always explains the other. For I live as a *philosopher* <note>original: "Philosophus"</note> and use the whole world instead of a book. From this—alongside Holy Scripture and through the illumination of **GOD**—I can grasp and comprehend the foundation of all truths and the true meaning of all secret writings.
